Combinatorial Reduction of Set Functions and Matroid Permutations through Minor Product Assignment
Mario Angelelli

Abstract
We introduce an algebraic model, based on the determinantal expansion of the product of two matrices, to test combinatorial reductions of set functions. Each term of the determinantal expansion is deformed through a monomial factor in d \mathchar 29028 indeterminates, whose exponents define a ℤ d \mathbb{\mathchar 29018}^{\mathchar 29028} -valued set function. By combining the Grassmann-Plücker relations for the two matrices, we derive a family of sparse polynomials, whose factorisation properties in a Laurent polynomial ring are studied and related to information-theoretic notions. Under a given genericity condition, we prove the equivalence between combinatorial reductions and determinantal expansions with invertible minor products; specifically, a deformation returns a determinantal expansion if and only if it is induced by a diagonal matrix of units in ℂ ⁡ ( 𝐭 ) \mathbb{\mathchar 28995}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29044}\delimiter 84054785 acting as a kernel in the original determinant expression. This characterisation supports the definition of a new method for checking and recovering combinatorial reductions for matroid permutations.



1
Introduction

A natural question about set functions regards conditions
that imply a form of complexity reduction: given sets
\mathcal{\mathchar 29016}
and
\mathchar 29005
, a family
\mathfrak{\mathchar 29016}
of subsets of
\mathcal{\mathchar 29016}

\mathchar 28681\mathchar 12346\,\mathfrak{\mathchar 29016}\mathrel{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathchar 512}$}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchar 545}\mathchar 29005

\mathchar 28960\mathchar 12346\,\mathcal{\mathchar 29016}\mathrel{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathchar 512}$}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchar 545}\mathchar 29005
such that, for all
\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}\mathchar 12850\mathfrak{\mathchar 29016}
, we can recover
\mathchar 28681\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}\delimiter 84054785
from

\{\mathchar 28960\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28939\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12346\,\mathchar 28939\mathchar 12850\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}\}
. The additivity axiom for countable probability distributions is a fundamental example of such a condition; but we can extend this investigation to more general cases where
\mathchar 29005
is a
\mathchar 29010
-module for a ring
\mathchar 29010

\mathchar 28681
from
\mathchar 28960
by taking advantage of algebraic relations.

This work focuses on determinant expansions as a means to encode information about set functions, relying on maximal minors of two
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29035\mathchar 8706\mathchar 29038\delimiter 84054785
-dimensional full-rank complex matrices to specify the type of combinatorial reduction. In fact, Grassmann-Plücker relations among these minors are algebraic conditions entailing a reduction for
\mathchar 29035
-forms on
\mathbb{\mathchar 28995}^{\mathchar 29038}

[
5
, Ch.3.1]
: formally, they characterise totally decomposable
\mathchar 29035
-forms
\delimiter 69640972_{\mathchar 29033_{\mathchar 28721}}\mathchar 8798\dots\mathchar 8798\delimiter 69640972_{\mathchar 29033_{\mathchar 29035}}\mathchar 12850\mathchar 4950\displaylimits^{\mathchar 29035}\mathbb{\mathchar 28995}^{\mathchar 29038}
, which are labelled by
\mathchar 29035

\{\mathchar 29033_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29033_{\mathchar 29035}\}\mathchar 12818\{\mathchar 28721\,\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29038\}
and are expressed as the wedge product of
\mathchar 28721
-forms
\delimiter 69640972_{\mathchar 29033_{\mathchar 28721}}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\delimiter 69640972_{\mathchar 29033_{\mathchar 29035}}
.

The motivation behind the study of such determinantal relations is the wide range of applications of combinatorial, algebraic, and geometric properties of determinantal varieties and Grassmann-Plücker relations. To elucidate the main notions underlying this research, we start by recalling the explicit connection between determinantal varieties and one of these application areas.

1.3
Overview of main results and implications for permutation testing

\mathbb{\mathchar 28995}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29044}\mathchar 24891\mathbf{\mathchar 29043}\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 68408078\mathchar 29001

\mathchar 29045\mathchar 12850\{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29028\}

Theorem
1
may fail when there are not enough non-vanishing minors of
\mathbf{\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29044}\delimiter 84054785

\mathbf{\mathchar 29010}
and for two columns of
\mathbf{\mathchar 29004}

\mathbf{\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84054785
is violated, we can find a counterexample to Theorem

in contradiction to (

More general independence structures associated with
\mathbf{\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84054785
require an additional study of minimal conditions that guarantee the combinatorial reduction (
13
), and we dedicate a separate work to this investigation.

The algebraic structure resulting from this model combines two quadratic contributions, namely the Grassmann-Plücker relations for two matrices in (
3
). This results in a set of quartic equations that constrain the form of the minors, and hence the deformations preserving the determinantal form. Our second contribution delves into the relations between the set of allowed deformations and the factorisation properties of polynomials derived from this combination of Grassmann-Plücker relations, in particular their squarefree decomposition. In turn, we will find that this squarefree decomposition leads to a characterisation of Shannon’s entropy for a Bernoulli random variable (see Remark
6
). These observations highlight an information-theoretic interpretation of the model:
\mathbf{\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29044}\delimiter 84054785
is a source of structural information represented by the matroid
\mathfrak{\mathchar 28999}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 84054785
, and a generic matrix
\mathbf{\mathchar 29010}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29044}\delimiter 84054785
is selected to explore this structure using accessible information (
12
).

The assumptions of Theorem
1
specify neither the form of
\mathchar 29031_{\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}}
nor the exponents
\mathchar 28681\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}\delimiter 84054785
, which makes the Cauchy-Binet expansion a
sparse polynomial

[
9
]
with a given upper bound for its sparsity. This argument does not rely upon the association
\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}\mathrel{\mathchar 567\mathchar 545}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29031_{\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28681\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 84054785
or potential reductions arising from the cancellation of terms in the expansion (
3
): if we are able to find an assignment of non-vanishing terms
\mathchar 29031_{\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}}
that is consistent with (
3
) and
the assumptions of Theorem
1
, then the reduction (
13
) follows.

The third contribution of this work exploits Theorem
1
and the previous observation, providing a verification protocol to check the existence and, if so, recover a given permutation of
\{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29038\}
when available information is only provided by the unlabelled collection of terms (
12
).
When the set function
\mathchar 28681
corresponds to a permutation
\widehat{\mathchar 28681}\mathchar 12346\,\mathfrak{\mathchar 28999}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 84054785\mathrel{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathchar 512}$}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchar 545}\mathfrak{\mathchar 28999}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 84054785
, Theorem
1
can be used to check if it is induced by a permutation
\widehat{\mathchar 28960}\mathchar 12346\,\{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29038\}\mathrel{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathchar 512}$}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchar 545}\{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29038\}
even when we do not have explicit information about
\mathchar 28681
, but only through the terms of a candidate determinantal expansion.

Theorem 2
.

Let
\mathchar 29035\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12850\mathbb{\mathchar 29006}
with
\mathchar 29035\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29038
and
\max\{\mathchar 29038\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29035\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035\}\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28725
, and
\widehat{\mathchar 28681}\mathchar 12346\,\mathfrak{\mathchar 28999}\mathrel{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathchar 512}$}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchar 545}\mathfrak{\mathchar 28999}
be a permutation of a matroid
\mathfrak{\mathchar 28999}
on
\{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29038\}
with rank
\mathchar 29035
.
Assume that each
\mathchar 29038
-tuple
\mathbf{\mathchar 29044}\mathchar 12850\mathbb{\mathchar 28995}^{\mathchar 29038}
returns a list, i.e. a tuple standing for an unlabelled multiset of
\#\mathfrak{\mathchar 28999}
complex numbers, which represent the candidate components of a determinantal expansion. Then, we can choose two
\mathchar 29038
-tuples
\mathbf{\mathchar 29044}_{\mathchar 29045}
,
\mathchar 29045\mathchar 12850\{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28722\}
, to verify the fulfilment of the assumptions of Theorem
1
, the existence of a permutation
\mathchar 28960
of
\{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29038\}
that induces
\mathchar 28681
and, in the affirmative case, recover
\mathchar 28960
and matrices
\mathbf{\mathchar 29025}
and
\mathbf{\mathchar 29041}
that generate the terms of the expansion.

Additional
a priori
knowledge of the domain of the entries of
\mathbf{\mathchar 29025}
, e.g.
\mathbb{\mathchar 29018}
or an algebraic number field, can be used to extend the previous argument, enabling specific methods to perform the verification based on less information. This is shown in Corollary
4
, where only two numbers suffice to verify complexity reduction and retrieve two candidate matrices that define the determinantal expansion, if they exist.

This application provides a complementary view to distance-based permutation testing (see e.g.
[
4
]
) and relates to the geometric interpretation of maximal minors, which serve as homogeneous coordinates, in the projectivization
\mathbb{\mathchar 29008}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 4950\displaylimits^{\mathchar 29035}\mathbb{\mathchar 28995}^{\mathchar 29038}\delimiter 84054785
, for
\mathchar 29035
-subspaces of
\mathbb{\mathchar 28995}^{\mathchar 29038}
associated with
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29035\mathchar 8706\mathchar 29038\delimiter 84054785
-dimensional full-rank matrices. Indeed, the expansion (
3
) arises in the study of principal angles between subspaces of a vector space
[
11
]
and consequent applications
[
6
,
3
]
. Theorem
2
allows us to deal with the lack of explicit information on coordinate labelling, which is a phenomenon that arises in unlabelled sensing or shuffled data
[
15
,
14
]
; furthermore, it extracts sufficient information to decompose candidate minor products, identifying in this way two subspaces involved in the determinantal expansion.

The assumption of monomial deformation of terms in the Cauchy-Binet expansion is equivalent to the invertibility in the ring
\mathbb{\mathchar 28995}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29044}\delimiter 84054785
of the non-vanishing principal minors of the composed matrix

\begin{\mathchar29040\mathchar29037\mathchar29025\mathchar29044\mathchar29042\mathchar29033\mathchar29048}\mathbf{\mathchar 28720}_{\mathchar 29035}&\mathbf{\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29044}\delimiter 84054785\\
\mathbf{\mathchar 29010}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29044}\delimiter 84054785&\mathbf{\mathchar 28720}_{\mathchar 29038}\end{\mathchar29040\mathchar29037\mathchar29025\mathchar29044\mathchar29042\mathchar29033\mathchar29048}

indexed by subsets
\{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035\}\mathchar 8795\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}
with
\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}\mathchar 12818\{\mathchar 29035\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29035\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29038\}
and
\#\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29035
(here
\mathbf{\mathchar 28720}_{\mathchar 29035}
denotes the
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29035\mathchar 8706\mathchar 29035\delimiter 84054785
-dimensional null matrix). This connection with non-constant, invertible minor assignment in a given ring is the basis for further studies.

1.4
Organisation of the paper

After Section
2
, where we fix the notation that will be used in the rest of the paper, we derive the polynomial equations that are the basis of our study (Section
3
) and the constraints on algebraic extensions (Section
4
) resulting from these equations. In Section
5
, we explore the constraints for non-vanishing minors in the three-term Grassmann-Plücker relations and describe allowed configurations (Subsections
5.1
-
5.2
). From these results, in Section
6
we prove Theorem
1
, also investigating the occurrence of multiple distinct configurations to assess the minimal dimensions that guarantee combinatorial reduction. We provide an application to permutations on matroids in Section
7
.

Remark 1
.

Any unimodular matrix

Remark 4
.

There are three basic, non-equivalent actions that can be carried out on the indices of
\mathchar 28959\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}\mathchar 12906_{\mathchar 28939\mathchar 28940}^{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29034}\delimiter 84054785
: the identity, the exchange of upper indices
\mathchar 29033\leftrightarrows\mathchar 29034
(equivalently, lower indices
\mathchar 28939\leftrightarrows\mathchar 28940
), and the change of basis
\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}\mathrel{\mathchar 567\mathchar 545}\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}_{\mathchar 28939}^{\mathchar 29033}
(equivalently,
\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}\mathrel{\mathchar 567\mathchar 545}\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}_{\mathchar 28940}^{\mathchar 29034}
). Other operations can be obtained from the composition of the previous ones: for instance,
\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}\mathrel{\mathchar 567\mathchar 545}\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}_{\mathchar 28939}^{\mathchar 29034}
is obtained from the composition of
\mathchar 29033\leftrightarrows\mathchar 29034
, the above-mentioned exchange of basis, and a second exchange
\mathchar 29033\leftrightarrows\mathchar 28939
.

Looking at the action of these transformations on the
\mathchar 29017
-terms, they are obtained from the composition of the inversion
\mathchar 29030_{\mathchar 29032}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29017\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12346\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29017\mathrel{\mathchar 567\mathchar 545}\mathchar 29017^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}
associated with
\mathchar 29033\leftrightarrows\mathchar 29034
, and the transformation
\mathchar 29030_{\delimiter 69640972}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29017\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12346\mathchar 12349\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29017
associated with
\mathchar 29033\leftrightarrows\mathchar 28939
as in (
48
). These two functions are involutions and can be matched with transpositions in the permutation group
\mathcal{\mathchar 29011}_{\mathchar 28723}
: the identity is assigned to the trivial permutation,
\mathchar 29030_{\mathchar 29032}
is assigned to the transposition
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28721\,\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84054785
, and
\mathchar 29030_{\delimiter 69640972}
is assigned to
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28722\,\mathchar 28723\delimiter 84054785
. In this way, a combination of these two functions corresponds to the product of the associated transpositions, and the different combinations are recovered by decomposing the elements in
\mathcal{\mathchar 29011}_{\mathchar 28723}
in terms of
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28721\,\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84054785
and
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28722\,\mathchar 28723\delimiter 84054785
. In particular, (
49
) comes from the composition
\mathchar 29030_{\mathchar 29032}\mathchar 8718\mathchar 29030_{\delimiter 69640972}\mathchar 8718\mathchar 29030_{\mathchar 29032}
, which is mapped to the product of transpositions
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28721\,\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28722\,\mathchar 28723\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28721\,\mathchar 28722\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28721\,\mathchar 28723\delimiter 84054785
.

Lemma 3
.

All observable
\mathchar 29017
-terms with basis
\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}
lie in the same quadratic extension of
\mathbb{\mathchar 28998}
.

Proof.

We can assume the existence of a radical term
\mathchar 29017_{\mathchar 28939\mathchar 28940}^{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29034}
, otherwise all the observable
\mathchar 29017
-terms lie in
\mathbb{\mathchar 28998}
and the thesis follows.
By Lemma
1
, we can focus on observable sets that satisfy (
50
) for the rest of the proof: being
\mathbb{\mathchar 28995}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29044}\delimiter 84054785

\mathchar 29040\mathchar 12850\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29042\delimiter 84267779

\mathchar 28996\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28996\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 29002}\delimiter 84054785
.
∎

A consequence of the previous proposition is that the set

of monomials appearing in

is uniquely characterised by any radical

.

Corollary 1
.

When

Proof.

\mathchar 28965_{\mathchar 28720}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28965
, that is,
\mathchar 29028_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 12906\mathchar 29028_{\mathchar 28721}
. We can repeat this argument considering the dual of the previous order, which coincides with the order induced by the valuation
\mathchar 28951_{\mathchar 28723}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 8705\delimiter 84054785
with respect to
\mathbf{\mathchar 29044}_{\mathchar 28723}
when it is non-empty, or equivalently considering maxima instead of minima. This corresponds to the transformations
\mathbf{\mathchar 29029}_{\mathchar 28721}\leftrightarrows\mathbf{\mathchar 29029}_{\mathchar 28723}
and
\mathchar 28965\leftrightarrows\mathchar 28721\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28965
, according to (
78
). Thus, we also get
\mathchar 28965_{\mathchar 28720}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28965
, which means
\mathchar 28965\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28965_{\mathchar 28720}\mathchar 12349{{\mathchar 28721\over\mathchar 28722}}
. This leads to (
84
) and does not satisfy
\#\mathrm{\mathchar 29011\mathchar 29045\mathchar 29040\mathchar 29040}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28996\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28722
. Therefore,
\mathchar 28996\mathchar 12349\hat{\mathchar 28994}_{\mathchar 28939\mathchar 28940}^{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29034}
and to obtain
\#\mathrm{\mathchar 29011\mathchar 29045\mathchar 29040\mathchar 29040}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28996\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28722
, we find two linearly dependent elements of
\hat{\mathchar 28959}\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}\mathchar 12906_{\mathchar 28939\mathchar 28940}^{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29034}\delimiter 84054785
, say
\mathchar 28997_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29027_{\mathchar 29011}\mathchar 8705\mathchar 28997_{\mathchar 28723}
, where
\mathchar 29027_{\mathchar 29011}\mathchar 12850\mathbb{\mathchar 28995}
satisfies
\mathchar 28996\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29027_{\mathchar 29011}\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 28997_{\mathchar 28723}^{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29027_{\mathchar 29011}\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 28997_{\mathchar 28723}\mathchar 28997_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28997_{\mathchar 28721}^{\mathchar 28722}
. This is a binomial if and only if
\mathchar 29027_{\mathchar 29011}\mathchar 12850\{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\}
.
∎

A possible issue arising from this process has been highlighted in
Example
3
. Before analysing it in more detail, the results of Proposition
2
and Lemma
4
suggest the following definition.

Definition 5
.

[
Type and class
of radical terms
]
We say that a configuration
\mathchar 28959\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}\mathchar 12906_{\mathchar 28939\mathchar 28940}^{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29034}\delimiter 84054785

(or the associated polynomials
\mathchar 28994_{\mathchar 28939\mathchar 28940}^{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29034}
and
\mathchar 29017_{\mathchar 28939\mathchar 28940}^{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29034}
)
is of
\mathchar 28999
-type
(generic type) if
\#\mathchar 28681\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28996\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12606\mathchar 28722
and is of

\mathchar 29011
-type
(singular type) if
\#\mathchar 28681\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28996\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28722
. A
\mathchar 28999
-type
configuration
\mathchar 28959\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}\mathchar 12906_{\mathchar 28939\mathchar 28940}^{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29034}\delimiter 84054785
is
class-
\mathrm{\mathchar 29001}
if
\mathchar 29009_{\mathchar 28939\mathchar 28940}^{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29034}
is a monomial, and it is
class-
\mathrm{\mathchar 29001\mathchar 29001}

if
\hat{\mathchar 29009}_{\mathchar 28939\mathchar 28940}^{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29034}

is a binomial (
81
).

We stress that the type of configuration is independent of the set
\mathchar 28959\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}\mathchar 12906_{\mathchar 28939\mathchar 28940}^{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29034}\delimiter 84054785
, since it depends only on
\mathchar 28996
, while this may not hold for the class, as shown in Example
3
.

Theorem 3
.

From any radical
\mathchar 29017_{\mathchar 28939\mathchar 28940}^{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29034}

we can recover a finite number

of monomial configurations

6
Proof of the main result and dimensional bounds

This section explores the constrained form of radical terms derived in Theorem
3
together with the relation (
39
) and the condition in Remark
3
. The details of this section improve the qualitative results of Theorem
3
with respect to the dimensional constraints in the presence of radical terms. A deeper analysis of the implications of (
39
) provides quantitative bounds and leads to a better understanding of the possible monomial combinations.

Lemma 5
.

Let
\mathchar 29017_{\mathchar 28939_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 28939_{\mathchar 28722}}^{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29034}
and
\mathchar 29017_{\mathchar 28939_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 28939_{\mathchar 28723}}^{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29034}
be radical, while
\mathchar 29017_{\mathchar 28939_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 28939_{\mathchar 28723}}^{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29034}\mathchar 12850\mathbb{\mathchar 28998}

Proposition 4
.

When

, for all

) implies

. Together with the assumption

, we also infer

, which implies

7
Combinatorial reduction from set permutations to element permutations

An implication of the previous results concerns the extraction of information from a given combinatorial structure: specifically, we can embed the permutations of
\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29038\delimiter 84267779
into the permutations of
\mathfrak{\mathchar 28999}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12818\mathchar 381_{\mathchar 29035}\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29038\delimiter 84267779
, which induce a relabelling of the collection
\{\mathchar 28673_{\mathbf{\mathchar 29004}}\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 24891\,\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}\mathchar 12850\mathfrak{\mathchar 28999}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 84054785\}
. This lets us take advantage of the apparent complexity arising from the embedding, while still allowing us to recover the original permutation and matrices that provide the decomposition.

We adapt each permutation
\widehat{\mathchar 28681}\mathchar 12346\,\mathfrak{\mathchar 28999}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 84054785\mathrel{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathchar 512}$}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchar 545}\mathfrak{\mathchar 28999}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 84054785
to our formalism through the composition

\mathchar 28681\mathchar 12346\mathchar 12349\mathtt{\mathchar 29001\mathchar 29038\mathchar 29028}\mathchar 8718\widehat{\mathchar 28681}\mathchar 12346\,\mathchar 381_{\mathchar 29035}\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29038\delimiter 84267779\mathrel{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\mathchar 512}$}\mkern-3.0mu\mathchar 545}\{\mathchar 28720\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28721\}^{\mathchar 29038}

(113)

where
\mathtt{\mathchar 29001\mathchar 29038\mathchar 29028}
is the map that associates each set with its indicator function. This defines a set function with values in
\mathbb{\mathchar 29018}^{\mathchar 29038}
. With these premises, we have the following consequence of Theorem

:

.

Proposition 6
.

Let there exist an observable set
\mathchar 28959\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}\delimiter 69640972_{\mathchar 28939\mathchar 28940}^{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29034}\delimiter 84054785
whose corresponding polynomial
\mathchar 28998_{\mathchar 28939\mathchar 28940}^{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29034}
in (
46
) has distinct roots. Then, starting from the family of minor products
\{\mathchar 29032\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12346\,\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}\mathchar 12850\mathfrak{\mathchar 28999}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 84054785\}
, each choice of a root of
\mathchar 28998_{\mathchar 28939\mathchar 28940}^{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29034}
for
\mathchar 29017_{\mathchar 28939\mathchar 28940}^{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29034}
determines all the
\mathchar 29017
-terms associated with observable sets.

Proof.

Let us assume that there exists an observable set
\mathchar 28959\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}\delimiter 69640972_{\mathchar 28939\mathchar 28940}^{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29034}\delimiter 84054785
such that both the roots of
\mathchar 28998_{\mathchar 28939\mathchar 28940}^{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29034}
, call them
\ddot{\mathchar 29017}_{\mathchar 28939\mathchar 28940}^{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29034}
and
\widehat{\mathchar 29017}_{\mathchar 28939\mathchar 28940}^{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29034}
with
\ddot{\mathchar 29017}_{\mathchar 28939\mathchar 28940}^{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29034}\mathrel{{{{\mathchar 566\mathchar 61}}}}\widehat{\mathchar 29017}_{\mathchar 28939\mathchar 28940}^{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29034}
, are compatible with the family
\{\mathchar 29032\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12346\,\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}\mathchar 12850\mathfrak{\mathchar 28999}\delimiter 67273472\mathbf{\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 84054785\}
. Then, consider any
\mathchar 28941\mathchar 12850\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}^{\mathtt{\mathchar 28995}}
such that
\mathchar 28959\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}\delimiter 69640972_{\mathchar 28939\mathchar 28941}^{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29034}\delimiter 84054785
and
\mathchar 28959\delimiter 67273472\mathcal{\mathchar 29001}\delimiter 69640972_{\mathchar 28940\mathchar 28941}^{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29034}\delimiter 84054785
are observable too: supposing that the same root
\mathchar 29017_{\mathchar 28940\mathchar 28941}^{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29034}
of
\mathchar 28998_{\mathchar 28940\mathchar 28941}^{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29034}
is associated with both the choices
\ddot{\mathchar 29017}_{\mathchar 28939\mathchar 28940}^{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29034}
and
\widehat{\mathchar 29017}_{\mathchar 28939\mathchar 28940}^{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29034}
for
\mathchar 29017_{\mathchar 28939\mathchar 28940}^{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29034}
, the aforementioned compatibility imposes that

\mathchar 28998_{\mathchar 28939\mathchar 28941}^{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29034}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 8704\ddot{\mathchar 29017}_{\mathchar 28939\mathchar 28940}^{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29034}\mathchar 8705\mathchar 29017_{\mathchar 28940\mathchar 28941}^{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29034}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28998_{\mathchar 28939\mathchar 28941}^{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29034}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 8704\widehat{\mathchar 29017}_{\mathchar 28939\mathchar 28940}^{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29034}\mathchar 8705\mathchar 29017_{\mathchar 28940\mathchar 28941}^{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29034}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720
can hold only if the arguments
\mathchar 8704\ddot{\mathchar 29017}_{\mathchar 28939\mathchar 28940}^{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29034}\mathchar 8705\mathchar 29017_{\mathchar 28940\mathchar 28941}^{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29034}
and
\mathchar 8704\widehat{\mathchar 29017}_{\mathchar 28939\mathchar 28940}^{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29034}\mathchar 8705\mathchar 29017_{\mathchar 28940\mathchar 28941}^{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29034}
are the distinct roots of the quadratic polynomial
\mathchar 28998_{\mathchar 28939\mathchar 28941}^{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 29034}
